<tr><td colspan="7">例 Landing the probe safely was a signal achievement for the young team.  探测器安全着陆是这支年轻团队非同寻常的成就。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 average</td><td>/ˈævərɪdʒ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>平庸的，一般的；平均数</td><td>an average student<br>成绩平平的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His work is average but it always arrives on time.  他的活儿一般，但总能按时交。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inferior</td><td>/ɪnˈfɪriər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>低等的，次等的</td><td>inferior in quality<br>质量较差</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Customers complained that the new fabric was clearly inferior.  顾客抱怨新面料明显质量较差。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mediocre</td><td>/ˌmiːdiˈoʊkər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>平庸的；质量中等偏下的</td><td>a mediocre performance<br>平庸的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The orchestra's mediocre performance disappointed listeners who had waited months for the concert.  乐团平庸的表演让等了几个月的听众很失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>lurid</b></td><td>/ˈlʊrɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>骇人的；（色彩）刺目的</td><td>lurid details<br>骇人的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The newspaper printed lurid details that upset the victim's family deeply.  报纸刊登了骇人的细节，令受害者家人深感不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 appalling</td><td>/əˈpɔːlɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>骇人的，糟糕透顶的</td><td>appalling living conditions<br>骇人的居住条件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The workers lived in appalling conditions close to the mine.  工人们住在矿区附近骇人的条件中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 atrocious</td><td>/əˈtroʊʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极坏的，极其残忍的</td><td>an atrocious crime<br>残暴的罪行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court described the killing as an atrocious crime.  法庭称这起杀人事件是极其残忍的罪行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dreadful</td><td>/ˈdredfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的，糟透的</td><td>a dreadful mistake<br>可怕的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had a dreadful headache and went home early.  她头疼得厉害，很早就回家了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frightful</td><td>/ˈfraɪtfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的；极糟的</td><td>a frightful accident<br>可怕的事故</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The storm did frightful damage to the small fishing harbor.  风暴给这个小渔港造成可怕的破坏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ghastly</td><td>/ˈɡæstli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的；惨白的</td><td>a ghastly discovery<br>可怕的发现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He turned a ghastly white when he heard the news.  他听到消息时脸色惨白。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gruesome</td><td>/ˈɡruːsəm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的，令人毛骨悚然的</td><td>gruesome details<br>可怕的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness described the gruesome scene in a shaking voice.  证人用颤抖的声音描述了那可怕的场面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hideous</td><td>/ˈhɪdiəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常丑陋的</td><td>a hideous mask<br>丑陋可怕的面具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They tore down the hideous concrete tower that blocked the view.  他们拆掉了那座挡住视野的丑陋混凝土塔楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 horrid</td><td>/ˈhɔːrəd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的，讨厌的</td><td>a horrid experience<br>糟糕的经历</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The flight home turned into a horrid experience.  回家的航班变成一段糟糕的经历。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 horrific</td><td>/hɔˈrɪfɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的，令人恐惧的</td><td>a horrific traffic accident<br>可怕的交通事故</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Survivors described the horrific scene inside the burning tunnel.  幸存者描述了燃烧隧道里可怕的场面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 macabre</td><td>/məˈkɑːbrə/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恐怖的</td><td>a macabre tale<br>恐怖的故事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He entertained the children with a macabre tale about a haunted mill.  他用一个关于鬼屋磨坊的恐怖故事逗孩子们开心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 monstrous</td><td>/ˈmɑːnstrəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的；骇人的</td><td>a monstrous wave<br>巨浪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A monstrous wave rose above the tiny fishing boat.  一个巨浪在小渔船上方涌起。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nightmarish</td><td>/ˈnaɪtˌmerɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>如噩梦般恐怖的</td><td>a nightmarish journey home<br>噩梦般的回家路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Crossing the flooded streets was a nightmarish experience.  穿过被水淹的街道是噩梦般的经历。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 agreeable</td><td>/əˈɡriːəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人愉悦的；相一致的</td><td>an agreeable companion<br>一个讨人喜欢的同伴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She found him an agreeable companion on the long train journey.  她觉得他是长途火车旅程中一位讨人喜欢的同伴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 appealing</td><td>/əˈpiːlɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>吸引人的</td><td>an appealing offer<br>有吸引力的提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company made an appealing offer that she could hardly refuse.  公司提出了一个她几乎无法拒绝的诱人条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 delicious</td><td>/dɪˈlɪʃəs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>美味的，可口的</td><td>a delicious meal<br>一顿美味的饭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The soup was so delicious that nobody left a drop.  这汤太可口，没人剩下一滴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 delightful</td><td>/dɪˈlaɪtfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人愉快的，宜人的</td><td>a delightful surprise<br>令人愉快的惊喜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent a delightful evening by the lake.  我们在湖边度过了一个愉快的夜晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 enjoyable</td><td>/ɪnˈdʒɔɪəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人愉快的，有趣的</td><td>an enjoyable evening<br>愉快的一晚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We had a thoroughly enjoyable evening at the concert.  我们在音乐会上度过了非常愉快的一晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 enticing</td><td>/enˈtaɪsɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>诱人的，吸引人的</td><td>an enticing aroma<br>诱人的香气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bakery sent an enticing smell down the whole street.  面包店让整条街都飘着诱人的香味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inviting</td><td>/ɪnˈvaɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>吸引人的，诱人的</td><td>an inviting warm fire<br>诱人的温暖炉火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cool lake looked especially inviting that afternoon.  那天下午凉爽的湖水看着格外诱人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pleasant</td><td>/ˈplezənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人愉快的；和善的</td><td>a pleasant surprise<br>令人愉快的惊喜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent a pleasant afternoon walking along the quiet river.  我们沿着静静的河边散步，度过了愉快的下午。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 satisfying</td><td>/ˈsætɪsˌfaɪɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人满意的；有说服力的</td><td>a satisfying explanation<br>令人满意的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one offered a satisfying answer to the reporter's question.  没人对记者的问题给出令人满意的答复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>machination</b></td><td>/ˌmæʃɪˈneɪʃn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>诡计</td><td>political machinations<br>政治阴谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Careful readers can trace the machinations that brought down the old regime.  细心的读者能追溯到使旧政权垮台的种种诡计。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conspiracy</td><td>/kənˈspɪrəsi/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>阴谋，共谋</td><td>a conspiracy to defraud<br>诈骗的共谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two officials were charged with conspiracy to hide the evidence.  两名官员被控共谋隐藏证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 design</td><td>/dɪˈzaɪn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>图谋，意图；设计</td><td>an evil design<br>邪恶的图谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He denied having any design against his former partner.  他否认对前合伙人有任何图谋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intrigue</td><td>/ˈɪntriːɡ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>阴谋；激起…的兴趣</td><td>political intrigue<br>政治阴谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Court records reveal decades of political intrigue behind the sudden fall of the minister.  法庭记录揭示了这位大臣突然倒台背后数十年的政治阴谋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 plot</td><td>/ˈplɑːt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>密谋，策划；情节</td><td>plot against the king<br>密谋反对国王</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Several officers plotted secretly to seize the northern ports.  几名军官暗中策划夺取北方港口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scheme</td><td>/ˈskiːm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>阴谋，计划</td><td>a scheme to defraud investors<br>诈骗投资者的阴谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They devised a scheme to avoid paying any taxes.  他们设计了一个逃税的计谋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 3

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>madcap</b></td><td>/ˈmædkæp/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>莽撞冲动的；莽撞冲动的人</td><td>a madcap adventure<br>一场冒险的疯狂之旅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a madcap would try to cross that river during the flood.  只有热爱冒险的疯子才会在洪水期试图渡过那条河。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 daredevil</td><td>/ˈderdevl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>大胆鲁莽的（人）</td><td>a daredevil stunt<br>一次鲁莽的特技</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The daredevil rider jumped his motorcycle over six parked cars.  那位鲁莽大胆的骑手驾摩托车跃过六辆停放的汽车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 madman</td><td>/ˈmædˌmæn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>疯子，狂人</td><td>drive like a madman<br>开车像个疯子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He shouted at the traffic like a complete madman.  他对着车流大喊，像个十足的疯子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 audacious</td><td>/ɔːˈdeɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>大胆的，（但往往）愚勇的；令人吃惊的</td><td>an audacious plan<br>大胆的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The general approved an audacious plan to cross the river at night.  将军批准了一个夜间渡河的大胆计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bold</td><td>/ˈboʊld/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>大胆的，勇敢的；醒目的</td><td>a bold move<br>大胆的举动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was a bold decision to leave a secure job.  辞去稳定的工作是个大胆的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brash</td><td>/bræʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚勇的，鲁莽的</td><td>a brash young executive<br>鲁莽的年轻主管</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His brash remarks offended nearly everyone at the dinner table.  他鲁莽的言论几乎冒犯了餐桌上的每个人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 overbold</del></td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈboʊld/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过于大胆的，鲁莽的</td><td>an overbold attack<br>过于冒险的进攻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His overbold plan impressed nobody on the cautious old committee.  他过于冒险的计划没打动谨慎的老委员会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overconfident</td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈkɑːnfɪdənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>过于自信的</td><td>grow overconfident after winning<br>赢后变得过于自信</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team became overconfident and lost the second match.  球队变得过于自信，输掉了第二场比赛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reckless</td><td>/ˈrekləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不考虑后果的，大胆鲁莽的</td><td>reckless driving<br>鲁莽驾驶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His reckless driving on the icy road caused a serious accident.  他在结冰路面上的鲁莽驾驶造成了一场严重事故。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 temerarious</del></td><td>/ˌtɛməˈrɛriəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>鲁莽的，胆大妄为的</td><td>a temerarious decision<br>一个鲁莽的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was temerarious of him to sail alone in that storm.  他在那场风暴中独自出海实在鲁莽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 careful</td><td>/ˈkerfəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谨慎的，仔细的</td><td>careful consideration<br>慎重考虑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Be careful when you cross this busy street at night.  夜里穿过这条繁忙的街道时要小心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cautious</td><td>/ˈkɔːʃəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>谨慎的，小心的</td><td>a cautious approach<br>谨慎的做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investors remained cautious until the election results were announced.  在选举结果公布前投资者一直很谨慎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 circumspect</td><td>/ˈsɜːrkəmspekt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谨慎的，小心的</td><td>remain circumspect in public<br>在公开场合保持谨慎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Diplomats stay circumspect in public and blunt behind closed doors.  外交官在公开场合谨慎，关起门来则直言不讳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 guarded</td><td>/ˈɡɑːrdəd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谨慎的，有保留的</td><td>a guarded response<br>有保留的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister gave a guarded welcome to the new proposal.  部长对新提案表示了有保留的欢迎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 heedful</del></td><td>/ˈhiːdfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>留心的，注意的</td><td>heedful of the warning<br>留意警告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heedful of the forecast, the climbers turned back early.  留意到天气预报，登山者提早返回了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prudent</td><td>/ˈpruːdnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的；小心谨慎的，审慎的</td><td>a prudent investment<br>一笔审慎的投资</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It would be prudent to save part of every paycheck.  每份工资都存下一部分才是明智之举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wary</td><td>/ˈweri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>警惕的，谨慎的</td><td>wary of strangers<br>对陌生人警惕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Children are taught to be wary of strangers offering free rides.  孩子们被教导要警惕主动提供搭车的陌生人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>magnitude</b></td><td>/ˈmæɡnɪtuːd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>重要，重大</td><td>of the first magnitude<br>头等重要</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Few people grasped the magnitude of the coming economic crisis.  很少有人明白即将到来的经济危机的严重程度。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 account</td><td>/əˈkaʊnt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>说明，解释；账户</td><td>give a full account<br>作出完整说明</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness gave a detailed account of what she had seen.  证人详细说明了她所看到的情况。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 consequence</td><td>/ˈkɑːnsəkwens/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>结果；重要性，价值</td><td>of little consequence<br>无关紧要</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The full consequences of the new policy will not be clear for years.  这项新政策的全部后果多年内都不会明朗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 importance</td><td>/ɪmˈpɔːrtəns/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>重要性，重大</td><td>a matter of great importance<br>极重要的事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nobody grasped the importance of that letter at first.  起初没人明白那封信的重要性。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 moment</td><td>/ˈmoʊmənt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>重要性，重大；瞬间</td><td>of great moment<br>极为重要</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The treaty was an event of real moment for both nations.  这项条约对两国都是意义重大的事件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 momentousness</del></td><td>/moʊˈmɛntəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>重大，重要性</td><td>the momentousness of the decision<br>该决定的重大意义</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Few present understood the momentousness of that quiet vote.  在场者少有人明白那次平静投票的重大。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 significance</td><td>/səɡˈnɪfɪkəns/</td><td>★★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>意义，含义；重要性</td><td>of great significance<br>意义重大</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Scholars still debate the significance of these ancient carvings.  学者们仍在争论这些古代雕刻的含义。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 weight</td><td>/ˈweɪt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>重要性，影响力；重量</td><td>carry great weight<br>极有影响力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her opinion carries considerable weight with the committee.  她的意见在委员会里很有分量。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 weightiness</del></td><td>/ˈweɪtinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>重要性，严肃性</td><td>the weightiness of the charge<br>指控的严重性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge stressed the weightiness of the evidence involved.  法官强调了所涉证据的重要性。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 insignificance</td><td>/ˌɪnsɪɡˈnɪfɪkəns/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>无关紧要，微不足道</td><td>fade into insignificance<br>变得微不足道</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Beside that discovery his earlier work sank into insignificance.  与那项发现相比，他早期的工作显得微不足道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 littleness</del></td><td>/ˈlɪtəlnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>微小；狭隘</td><td>the littleness of their concerns<br>他们关切之事的琐小</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The vast sky reminded him of his own littleness.  广阔的天空让他想到自己的渺小。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 puniness</del></td><td>/ˈpjuːninəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>弱小，微不足道</td><td>the puniness of the effort<br>这份努力的微弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The puniness of the budget doomed the project early.  预算的微薄让项目早早注定失败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 slightness</del></td><td>/ˈslaɪtnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>轻微，细小</td><td>the slightness of the change<br>变化的轻微</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The slightness of the difference surprised the researchers.  差异之微小让研究者们意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 smallness</td><td>/ˈsmɔːlnəs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>微小，狭小</td><td>the smallness of the room<br>房间的狭小</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Visitors always comment on the smallness of the chapel.  来访者总会说起这座小教堂的窄小。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 triviality</td><td>/ˌtrɪviˈæləti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>琐事；微不足道</td><td>argue over trivialities<br>为琐事争吵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The long meeting sank into arguments about pure trivialities.  漫长的会议陷入了对纯粹琐事的争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>malevolent</b></td><td>/məˈlevələnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶意的，恶毒的</td><td>a malevolent glare<br>恶毒的目光</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old man gave the boys a malevolent glare and slammed the gate.  老人恶毒地瞪了那些男孩一眼，砰地关上了大门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cruel</td><td>/ˈkruːəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>残酷的，无情的</td><td>a cruel joke<br>残忍的玩笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was cruel to leave the dog out in the storm.  把狗留在暴风雨里太残忍了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 despiteful</del></td><td>/dɪˈspaɪtfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶意的，怀恨的</td><td>a despiteful remark<br>一句恶意的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her despiteful comments hurt him more than she realized.  她恶意的评论比她意识到的更伤他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 evil</td><td>/ˈiːvəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>邪恶；邪恶的</td><td>the lesser evil<br>较小的祸害</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel examines how ordinary people come to commit evil.  这部小说探讨普通人如何走向作恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 malicious</td><td>/məˈlɪʃəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶意的</td><td>a malicious rumor<br>恶意的谣言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A malicious rumor about the candidate spread quickly across social media.  一条关于这位候选人的恶意谣言在社交媒体上迅速传播。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 malign</td><td>/məˈlaɪn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>恶意的；诽谤</td><td>malign his motives<br>诋毁他的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rival newspapers began to malign the senator without offering a shred of evidence.  竞争对手的报纸开始诽谤这位参议员，却拿不出一丝证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 malignant</td><td>/məˈlɪɡnənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶毒的，邪恶的</td><td>a malignant rumor<br>恶毒的谣言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A malignant rumor about the coach spread through the school within a single afternoon.  一则关于教练的恶毒谣言一个下午就在学校里传开了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mean</td><td>/miːn/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑贱的；吝啬的</td><td>a mean little room<br>简陋卑贱的小屋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The family lived in a mean little room above a noisy printing shop.  一家人住在嘈杂印刷店楼上一间简陋的小屋里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nasty</td><td>/ˈnæsti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的，肮脏的；恶劣的</td><td>a nasty smell<br>难闻的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A nasty stain spread slowly across the pale carpet.  一块难看的污渍在浅色地毯上慢慢蔓延。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spiteful</td><td>/ˈspaɪtfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶意的，怀恨的</td><td>a spiteful remark<br>一句恶毒的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She sent a spiteful message to her former business partner.  她给前生意伙伴发了一条恶毒的消息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vicious</td><td>/ˈvɪʃəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>凶恶的，恶毒的</td><td>a vicious circle<br>恶性循环</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dog gave a vicious bite that required several stitches.  那只狗凶恶地咬了一口，需要缝几针。</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 virulent</td><td>/ˈvɪrələnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶毒的，充满敌意的；毒性强的</td><td>a virulent personal attack<br>恶毒的人身攻击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The columnist launched a virulent attack on the new mayor.  这位专栏作家对新市长发起了恶毒的攻击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 benevolent</td><td>/bəˈnevələnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>仁慈的，行善的</td><td>a benevolent employer<br>仁慈的雇主</td></tr>

<tr><td>↳近 contrive</td><td>/kənˈtraɪv/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>谋划，设法做到；发明</td><td>contrive a meeting<br>设法安排一次会面</td></tr>

<tr><td colspan="7">例 She handled the awkward question with real finesse and everyone relaxed.  她娴熟巧妙地处理了那个尴尬的问题，大家都放松了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frame</td><td>/ˈfreɪm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>制定，构想；框架</td><td>frame a new policy<br>制定新政策</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee framed a new policy on remote work overnight.  委员会一夜之间制定了新的远程工作政策。</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 machinate</del></td><td>/ˈmækəˌneɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>阴谋策划，图谋</td><td>machinate against a rival<br>图谋对付对手</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 manipulate</td><td>/məˈnɪpjuleɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>巧妙处理；暗中操控</td><td>manipulate public opinion<br>操控公众舆论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The campaign tried to manipulate public opinion by releasing carefully edited video clips.  竞选团队试图通过发布精心剪辑的视频片段来操控公众舆论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mastermind</td><td>/ˈmæstərˌmaɪnd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>策划，幕后操纵</td><td>mastermind the whole scheme<br>策划整个计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two former employees masterminded the elaborate bank robbery.  两名前员工策划了这起精心设计的银行劫案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 negotiate</td><td>/nɪˈɡoʊʃieɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>商量，谈判</td><td>negotiate a contract<br>谈判一份合同</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two sides negotiated a contract that satisfied neither of them completely.  双方谈成了一份合同，但双方都不完全满意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wangle</td><td>/ˈwæŋɡəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>巧妙谋得，设法弄到</td><td>wangle an invitation<br>设法弄到一张邀请函</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wangled two days off in the busiest week.  她在最忙的一周里设法弄到两天假。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 botch</td><td>/ˈbɑːtʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>笨拙地弄糟，搞坏</td><td>botch the job<br>把活儿搞糟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The builders botched the job and had to start over.  建筑工人把活儿搞糟了，只能重来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bungle</td><td>/ˈbʌŋɡl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>办糟，失败</td><td>bungle the job<br>把工作办糟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crew bungled the repair and flooded the entire basement.  工人把维修办糟了，整个地下室都被水淹了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fumble</td><td>/ˈfʌmbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>笨拙地做</td><td>fumble for the keys<br>笨拙地摸找钥匙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He fumbled for the keys in his dark coat pocket.  他在昏暗的外套口袋里笨拙地摸找钥匙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mishandle</td><td>/mɪsˈhændəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>处理不当，胡乱对待</td><td>mishandle the negotiations<br>把谈判处理得很糟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Officials badly mishandled the response to the flood.  官员们对洪水的应对处理得极糟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 muff</td><td>/ˈmʌf/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>笨拙地做；漏接（球）</td><td>muff a catch<br>漏接一个球</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nervous fielder managed to muff an easy catch in the ninth inning.  这名紧张的外野手在第九局漏接了一个容易的球。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 scamp</td><td>/skæmp/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>淘气鬼；无赖</td><td>a cheerful little scamp<br>快活的小淘气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That scamp hid my glasses in the flour jar again.  那个小淘气又把我的眼镜藏进面粉罐里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mess up</td><td>/mɛs ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>弄糟，搞乱</td><td>mess up the plan<br>把计划搞乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not mess up the report the night before the deadline.  别在截止前夜把报告搞砸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>marginal</b></td><td>/ˈmɑːrdʒɪnl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不重要的</td><td>of marginal importance<br>无关紧要的</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee dismissed the objection as marginal and moved on to weightier matters.  委员会认为这项反对无关紧要，转而讨论更重要的事情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inconsequential</td><td>/ˌɪnkɑːnsɪˈkwenʃəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不重要的，微不足道的</td><td>an inconsequential remark<br>无关紧要的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The difference between the two prices is quite inconsequential.  两个价格之间的差别微不足道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inconsiderable</td><td>/ˌɪnkənˈsɪdərəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>微不足道的</td><td>an inconsiderable sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The risk is small but by no means inconsiderable.  风险虽小，却绝非微不足道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insignificant</td><td>/ˌɪnsɪɡˈnɪfɪkənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关紧要的，微不足道的</td><td>a seemingly insignificant change<br>看似微小的改动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An insignificant error early on ruined the entire calculation.  早期一个微不足道的错误毁了整个计算。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 minor</td><td>/ˈmaɪnər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>较小的，不重要的</td><td>a minor problem<br>一个小问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only minor repairs were needed after the small accident.  小事故后只需要一些小修理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 minute</td><td>/maɪˈnjuːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极小的，微不足道的；细致的</td><td>a minute quantity<br>极小的量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even a minute trace of the chemical can spoil the batch.  即使极微量的这种化学品也会毁掉整批产品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 negligible</td><td>/ˈneɡlədʒəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>微不足道的，可忽略的</td><td>a negligible difference<br>可忽略的差异</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The extra cost is negligible compared with the savings.  与节省的钱相比，额外成本微不足道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 nugatory</del></td><td>/ˈnuːɡətɔːri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关紧要的</td><td>a nugatory objection<br>无关紧要的反对</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court dismissed his nugatory objection and proceeded with the hearing.  法庭驳回了他无关紧要的反对，继续进行听证。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slight</td><td>/slaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>轻微的，不重要的；轻视，怠慢</td><td>slight her contribution<br>轻视她的贡献</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt slighted when nobody mentioned his contribution during the ceremony.  典礼上无人提及他的贡献，他觉得受到了轻视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trifling</td><td>/ˈtraɪflɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>细微的，不重要的</td><td>a trifling sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He dismissed the missing coins as a trifling sum not worth reporting.  他把丢失的硬币当作微不足道的小钱，不值得上报。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trivial</td><td>/ˈtrɪviəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无足轻重的，不重要的</td><td>a trivial detail<br>无关紧要的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor refused to argue over such a trivial difference in wording.  编辑不愿为措辞上如此无足轻重的差别争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 consequential</td><td>/ˌkɑːnsəˈkwenʃəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的；随之而来的</td><td>a consequential ruling<br>一项重要的裁决</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That quiet vote proved the most consequential act of the session.  那次不起眼的投票成了本届会议最重要的举动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 eventful</td><td>/ɪˈventfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>多事的，重要的</td><td>an eventful year<br>多事的一年</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It has been an eventful year for the whole family.  对全家来说这是多事的一年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 important</td><td>/ˌɪmˈpɔːrtənt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的，重大的</td><td>an important decision<br>一个重要的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sleep is as important as diet for a young athlete.  对年轻运动员来说，睡眠和饮食一样重要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 meaningful</td><td>/ˈmiːnɪŋfəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有意义的，意味深长的</td><td>meaningful work<br>有意义的工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She left banking to find more meaningful work.  她离开银行业去寻找更有意义的工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 momentous</td><td>/moʊˈmentəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>重大的，重要的</td><td>a momentous decision<br>一个重大的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board reached a momentous decision that changed the company's direction entirely.  董事会做出了一个重大决定，彻底改变了公司的方向。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 significant</td><td>/səɡˈnɪfɪkənt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的；显著的</td><td>a significant difference<br>显著的差别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Researchers found a significant drop in the pollution levels.  研究者发现污染水平显著下降。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 substantial</td><td>/səbˈstænʃl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>大量的，可观的；实质的</td><td>a substantial increase<br>大幅的增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company reported a substantial increase in profits this quarter.  公司报告本季度利润大幅增长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 weighty</td><td>/ˈweɪti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的，重大的；沉重的</td><td>weighty matters<br>重大的事务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The elders debated weighty questions of land and water.  长者们讨论着土地与水源的重大问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>meditate</b></td><td>/ˈmedɪteɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>思索，沉思</td><td>meditate on the problem<br>沉思这个问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He sat by the window and meditated on the day's events.  他坐在窗边沉思白天发生的事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 cogitate</del></td><td>/ˈkɑːdʒɪˌteɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>认真思考，仔细考虑</td><td>cogitate on the problem<br>仔细思考这个问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He sat by the window and cogitated on his next move.  他坐在窗边，仔细思量下一步该怎么走。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 consider</td><td>/kənˈsɪdər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>仔细考虑，斟酌</td><td>consider the options carefully<br>仔细斟酌各种选择</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She considered the options carefully before signing the lease.  她在签租约前仔细斟酌了各种选择。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 contemplate</td><td>/ˈkɑːntəmpleɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>沉思，仔细思索</td><td>contemplate the consequences<br>仔细思索后果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She sat by the window and contemplated the few choices left to her.  她坐在窗边，仔细思索留给自己的几个选择。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deliberate</td><td>/dɪˈlɪbərət/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深思熟虑的，故意的</td><td>a deliberate attempt<br>故意的尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The delay was deliberate, designed to weaken our position.  这次延误是故意的，目的是削弱我们的立场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mull</td><td>/ˈmʌl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>反复思考，仔细琢磨</td><td>mull over the offer<br>反复考虑这个提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He mulled over the decision for several sleepless nights.  他为这个决定辗转思考了好几个夜晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 perpend</del></td><td>/pərˈpɛnd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>仔细考虑，斟酌</td><td>perpend the evidence<br>仔细斟酌证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge asked the jury to perpend the evidence overnight.  法官要陪审团用一夜时间仔细斟酌证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ponder</td><td>/ˈpɑːndər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>仔细考虑，深思</td><td>ponder the question<br>细想这个问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He pondered the offer for a week before finally accepting it.  他把这个提议考虑了一周才最终接受。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ruminate</td><td>/ˈruːmɪneɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>沉思；反复琢磨；反刍</td><td>ruminate on the past<br>反复思量往事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He sat by the window and ruminated on the past.  他坐在窗边，反复回想往事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 study</td><td>/ˈstʌdi/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>学习，研究</td><td>study hard for exams<br>为考试用功</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She studies medieval history at a university in Scotland.  她在苏格兰一所大学研究中世纪史。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 weigh</td><td>/ˈweɪ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>权衡，仔细考虑</td><td>weigh the risks<br>权衡各种风险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They weighed the risks for several hours before agreeing.  他们权衡风险数小时后才达成一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wrestle</td><td>/ˈresəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>全力思索，苦苦应对</td><td>wrestle with the question<br>苦苦思索这个问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Philosophers still wrestle with the nature of consciousness.  哲学家们仍在苦苦思索意识的本质。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disregard</td><td>/ˌdɪsrɪˈɡɑːrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>漠视，不关注；缺乏兴趣，缺乏关心</td><td>total disregard for safety<br>完全不顾安全</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their total disregard for safety rules eventually caused a serious accident.  他们完全不顾安全规定，最终造成了一起严重事故。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ignore</td><td>/ˌɪɡˈnɔːr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>忽视，不理</td><td>ignore the warning<br>忽视警告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The captain ignored the warning and sailed into the storm.  船长忽视了警告，驶入了风暴之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 overlook</td><td>/ˈoʊvərˌlʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>忽视；宽恕；俯视</td><td>overlook a minor error<br>宽容小错</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher chose to overlook the boy's late arrival.  老师决定不追究那男孩的迟到。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 slight</td><td>/slaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>轻微的，不重要的；轻视，怠慢</td><td>slight her contribution<br>轻视她的贡献</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt slighted when nobody mentioned his contribution during the ceremony.  典礼上无人提及他的贡献，他觉得受到了轻视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>menial</b></td><td>/ˈmiːniəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑贱的，低下的</td><td>menial tasks<br>低贱的杂活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She took menial jobs to pay for night school.  她做低微的工作来支付夜校学费。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 base</td><td>/ˈbeɪs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑劣的，低下的</td><td>base motives<br>卑劣的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He acted from base motives of greed and envy.  他出于贪婪和嫉妒的卑劣动机行事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 humble</td><td>/ˈhʌmbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑微的，地位低下的；谦逊的</td><td>humble origins<br>卑微的出身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rose from humble origins to lead the company.  他出身卑微，最终领导了这家公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lowly</td><td>/ˈloʊli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>地位低的，卑微的</td><td>a lowly clerk<br>一个卑微的职员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He began as a lowly assistant and rose to direct the firm.  他从卑微的助理做起，后来掌管了公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 servile</td><td>/ˈsɜːrvl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑屈的，奴性的</td><td>servile flattery<br>奴性的谄媚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His servile manner toward the boss annoyed everyone.  他对老板卑躬屈膝的态度让大家反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slavish</td><td>/ˈsleɪvɪʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>盲从的，奴性的</td><td>slavish imitation<br>盲目的模仿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The design is a slavish imitation of an older model.  这个设计是对旧款的盲目模仿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 subservient</td><td>/səbˈsɜːrviənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>奉承的，屈从的</td><td>subservient to his boss<br>对上司百般奉承</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She refused to be subservient to anyone merely for a promotion.  她拒绝为了升职而对任何人卑躬屈膝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 arrogant</td><td>/ˈærəɡənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，自大的</td><td>an arrogant manner<br>傲慢的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His arrogant remarks cost him the respect of his colleagues.  他傲慢的言论使他失去了同事的尊重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 haughty</td><td>/ˈhɔːti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，目中无人的</td><td>a haughty manner<br>傲慢的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave the waiter a haughty look and said nothing.  她傲慢地看了服务员一眼，什么也没说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 imperious</td><td>/ɪmˈpɪriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，专横的</td><td>an imperious tone<br>专横的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her imperious manner made junior colleagues afraid to disagree.  她专横的态度让年轻同事不敢有异议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lordly</td><td>/ˈlɔːrdli/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的；高贵的</td><td>a lordly manner<br>傲慢的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He waved the waiter away with a lordly gesture.  他以傲慢的手势把侍者挥退。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 supercilious</td><td>/ˌsuːpərˈsɪliəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>高傲的，傲慢的</td><td>a supercilious smile<br>傲慢的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A supercilious smile crossed his face when he heard my question.  听到我的问题，他脸上掠过傲慢的微笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 superior</td><td>/suːˈpɪriər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>更好的，占优势的；上级</td><td>superior to the rest<br>优于其余各方</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This model is clearly superior to the older version.  这个型号明显优于旧版。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>meretricious</b></td><td>/ˌmerəˈtrɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>艳俗的，俗气的</td><td>meretricious ornament<br>艳俗的装饰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hotel lobby was crowded with meretricious ornaments of gilded plaster and plastic.  酒店大堂里堆满了镀金石膏和塑料做的艳俗装饰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flamboyant</td><td>/flæmˈbɔɪənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>艳丽夺目的，炫耀的，充满装饰的</td><td>a flamboyant style<br>浮夸炫目的风格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The singer wore a flamboyant coat covered in mirrors and feathers.  歌手穿着一件缀满镜片和羽毛的艳丽夺目的外套。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flaring</td><td>/ˈflerɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>耀眼张扬的；摇曳燃烧的</td><td>flaring colors<br>耀眼的色彩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dancer's flaring costume drew every eye in the theater.  舞者张扬耀眼的服装吸引了剧场里所有的目光。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flashy</td><td>/ˈflæʃi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>浮华的，华而不实的</td><td>a flashy sports car<br>浮华的跑车</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived in a flashy suit that everyone immediately noticed.  他穿着一身浮华的西装到场，所有人立刻都注意到了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 garish</td><td>/ˈɡerɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过于鲜艳的，过于张扬的</td><td>garish colors<br>过于鲜艳的颜色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lobby was painted in garish colors that hurt the eyes.  大堂被漆成刺眼的过于鲜艳的颜色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gaudy</td><td>/ˈɡɔːdi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>俗丽的</td><td>gaudy jewelry<br>俗丽的首饰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wore gaudy jewelry that clashed with her simple dress.  她戴着与朴素连衣裙不搭的俗丽首饰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glaring</td><td>/ˈɡlerɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，显眼的；耀眼的</td><td>a glaring error<br>明显的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a glaring error on the very first printed page.  印刷的第一页上就有一个明显错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 loud</td><td>/ˈlaʊd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>大声的，响亮的</td><td>a loud bang<br>一声巨响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The music was too loud for normal conversation.  音乐太响，无法正常交谈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ostentatious</td><td>/ˌɑːstenˈteɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>炫耀卖弄的</td><td>ostentatious display of wealth<br>炫富的排场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His ostentatious diamond watch drew stares from everyone in the modest room.  他炫耀的钻石表引来了朴素房间里所有人的注视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tawdry</td><td>/ˈtɔːdri/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>俗丽的，花哨而庸俗的</td><td>tawdry decorations<br>花哨庸俗的装饰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tawdry decorations covered the walls of the cheap seaside hotel.  花哨庸俗的装饰铺满廉价海滨旅馆的墙壁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conservative</td><td>/kənˈsɜːrvətɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>守旧的，不愿改变的；不招摇的，低调的</td><td>a conservative estimate<br>保守的估计</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her parents were too conservative to accept such an unconventional career choice.  她的父母太守旧，无法接受这样不合常规的职业选择。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 quiet</td><td>/ˈkwaɪət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>安静的；平静的</td><td>keep the room quiet<br>保持房间安静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The library stays quiet even during exam week.  即使在考试周，图书馆也保持安静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 understated</td><td>/ˈʌndərˌsteɪtɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>含蓄的，不张扬的</td><td>understated elegance<br>含蓄的优雅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her understated gray suit suited the solemn occasion perfectly.  她那身低调的灰西装非常适合这个庄重的场合。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unflamboyant</del></td><td>/ˌʌnflæmˈbɔɪənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不张扬的，朴素的</td><td>an unflamboyant style<br>不张扬的风格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His unflamboyant suits contrasted with his loud opinions.  他朴素的西装与他张扬的观点形成对比。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>milieu</b></td><td>/miːlˈjuː/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>环境，氛围</td><td>social milieu<br>社会环境</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She never felt comfortable in the social milieu of her husband's wealthy family.  她在丈夫富有家族的社交环境中从未感到自在。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ambient</td><td>/ˈæmbiənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>周围的，环境的</td><td>ambient temperature<br>环境温度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cafe's ambient lighting made the whole room feel warm and inviting.  咖啡馆的环境灯光让整个房间显得温馨宜人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 atmosphere</td><td>/ˈætməsˌfɪr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>气氛，情调</td><td>a tense atmosphere<br>紧张的气氛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A tense atmosphere filled the room before the verdict.  宣判前，屋里弥漫着紧张的气氛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 climate</td><td>/ˈklaɪmət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>风气，氛围；气候</td><td>the political climate<br>政治气候</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The current political climate discourages honest debate.  当前的政治气候不利于诚实的辩论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 context</td><td>/ˈkɑːntekst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>背景，环境；上下文</td><td>in a historical context<br>在历史背景下</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 You cannot judge her remark without knowing the context in which it appeared.  不了解这句话出现的语境，你无法评判它。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 environment</td><td>/ɪnˈvaɪrənmənt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>环境，周围状况</td><td>a safe working environment<br>安全的工作环境</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Children thrive in an environment where they feel both safe and respected.  孩子在既安全又受尊重的环境中才能成长得好。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 environs</td><td>/ɪnˈvaɪrənz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>周边地区，近郊</td><td>the environs of the city<br>城市周边地区</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Farms still cover much of the environs of the old capital.  农田仍覆盖着这座古都的大部分近郊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 medium</td><td>/ˈmiːdiəm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>擅长的表现形式；媒介</td><td>her favorite medium<br>她最爱的表现媒介</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Watercolor is the medium in which he works best.  水彩是他最擅长的表现媒介。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 setting</td><td>/ˈsetɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（事件的）环境，背景</td><td>a rural setting<br>乡村环境</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel's setting is a small fishing village on the Irish coast.  这部小说的背景是爱尔兰海岸的一个小渔村。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 surroundings</td><td>/səˈraʊndɪŋz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>周围环境，四周</td><td>unfamiliar surroundings<br>陌生的环境</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cat spent an hour cautiously exploring its new surroundings.  猫花了一小时小心地探索新环境。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 terrain</td><td>/təˈreɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>地形，地势</td><td>rough mountain terrain<br>复杂的山地地形</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rescue team struggled across rocky terrain to reach the injured hiker.  救援队艰难穿越岩石地形去救受伤的徒步者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 4

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>misapprehension</b></td><td>/ˌmɪsæprɪˈhenʃn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>错误的理解、判断</td><td>under a misapprehension<br>有误解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 You are under a misapprehension if you think the deadline has been extended.  如果你以为截止日期延长了，那你是误解了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 incomprehension</td><td>/ˌɪnkɑːmprɪˈhenʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不理解，无法领悟</td><td>a look of incomprehension<br>困惑不解的表情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stared at the equations with total incomprehension and quietly closed the textbook.  他一脸茫然地盯着方程式，悄悄合上了教科书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 misconstruction</del></td><td>/ˌmɪskənˈstrʌkʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>误解，曲解</td><td>open to misconstruction<br>易被曲解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His careless wording was open to misconstruction by anyone reading it quickly.  他措辞随意，任何草草阅读的人都可能曲解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 misconstruing</del></td><td>/ˌmɪskənˈstruːɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>误解，曲解</td><td>misconstruing his intentions<br>误解他的意图</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Misconstruing her silence as agreement, he went ahead and signed the contract.  他把她的沉默误解为同意，就径自签了合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 misimpression</del></td><td>/mɪsɪmˈpreʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>错误印象</td><td>create a misimpression<br>造成错误印象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The headline created a misimpression that the drug had already been approved.  这条标题造成了一种错误印象，好像该药已获批准。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 misinterpretation</td><td>/mɪsɪnˌtɜːrprəˈteɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>误解，错误解释</td><td>open to misinterpretation<br>易被误解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The vague instructions left plenty of room for misinterpretation by new employees.  含糊的说明给新员工留下了很大的误解空间。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 misknowledge</del></td><td>/ˌmɪsˈnɑːlɪdʒ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>错误的认识，谬知</td><td>spread misknowledge<br>传播错误认识</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rumor and misknowledge spread faster online than any careful correction ever could.  谣言和错误认识在网上传播得比任何认真的更正都快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 misreading</td><td>/mɪsˈriːdɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>误读，误解</td><td>a misreading of the data<br>对数据的误读</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her whole argument rests on a misreading of the original survey data.  她的整个论点都建立在对原始调查数据的误读之上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 misunderstanding</td><td>/ˌmɪsəndərˈstændɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>误解，误会</td><td>a simple misunderstanding<br>一场简单误会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole quarrel grew from one small misunderstanding.  整场争吵源于一个小小的误会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 miscalculation</td><td>/ˌmɪskælkjəˈleɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>误算，估计错误</td><td>a serious miscalculation<br>严重的误判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single miscalculation in the budget cost the department nearly a million dollars.  预算中的一处误算让该部门损失了近百万美元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 misjudging</td><td>/mɪsˈdʒʌdʒɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>误判，估计错误</td><td>misjudging the distance<br>误判距离</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Misjudging the distance, the driver braked far too late and clipped the fence.  司机误判了距离，刹车太晚，刮到了栅栏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 misjudgment</td><td>/mɪsˈdʒʌdʒmənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>判断失误，误判</td><td>a costly misjudgment<br>代价高昂的误判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Hiring him proved to be the most expensive misjudgment of her career.  雇用他被证明是她职业生涯中代价最高的一次误判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 misstep</td><td>/mɪsˈstep/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>失误，差错；失足</td><td>a serious misstep<br>严重的失误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One misstep in the negotiations could undo three years of patient diplomacy.  谈判中一次失误就可能毁掉三年的耐心外交。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slip</td><td>/ˈslɪp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>溜走；滑倒</td><td>slip through the crowd<br>从人群中溜过</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She slipped quietly out of the meeting before the vote.  她在投票前悄悄溜出了会议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 slipup</del></td><td>/ˈslɪpʌp/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>差错；小疏忽</td><td>a minor slipup<br>小小的失误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One tiny slipup in the calculation cost the company several thousand dollars last month.  计算中一个小小的差错上月让公司损失了数千美元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>monarch</b></td><td>/ˈmɑːnərk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>君主，帝王</td><td>an absolute monarch<br>专制君主</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 For forty years the country was ruled by an absolute monarch.  四十年间这个国家由一位专制君主统治。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 autocrat</td><td>/ˈɔːtəˌkræt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>独裁者，专制统治者</td><td>rule like an autocrat<br>像独裁者一样统治</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The founder ran his company like a benevolent autocrat.  创始人像一位仁慈的独裁者那样经营公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lord</td><td>/ˈlɔːrd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>主人，领主；贵族</td><td>lord of the manor<br>庄园领主</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tenants paid rent to the lord of the manor each autumn.  佃户每年秋天向庄园领主交租。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 potentate</td><td>/ˈpoʊtnteɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>有权势的人</td><td>an Eastern potentate<br>一位东方权贵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ancient potentate ruled his desert kingdom with absolute and unquestioned authority.  那位古代权贵以绝对且不容置疑的权威统治他的沙漠王国。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ruler</td><td>/ˈruːlər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>统治者，君主</td><td>a wise ruler<br>贤明的统治者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 History remembers him as a wise and patient ruler.  历史将他记作一位贤明而有耐心的统治者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sovereign</td><td>/ˈsɑːvrən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>拥有最高权力的；君主</td><td>a sovereign state<br>主权国家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The island became a sovereign nation after years of struggle.  经过多年抗争，该岛成为主权国家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>monopolize</b></td><td>/məˈnɑːpəlaɪz/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>垄断，主宰</td><td>monopolize the conversation<br>独占谈话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One guest managed to monopolize the conversation for the entire length of dinner.  一位客人在整个晚餐期间独占了谈话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 control</td><td>/kənˈtroʊl/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>控制权，支配；控制</td><td>seize control of<br>夺取…的控制权</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rebels seized control of the capital within a single night.  叛军一夜之间就夺取了首都的控制权。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dominate</td><td>/ˈdɑːməˌneɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>支配，统治；俯视</td><td>dominate the market<br>支配市场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two firms dominate the industry and set nearly every price.  两家公司支配着这个行业，几乎决定所有价格。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 govern</td><td>/ˈɡʌvərn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>控制，支配；统治</td><td>govern his temper<br>控制他的脾气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Strict rules govern how the samples must be stored.  严格的规定约束着样本必须如何保存。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reign</td><td>/reɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>统治权</td><td>during her reign<br>在她统治期间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Trade with distant ports flourished during her long and peaceful reign.  在她漫长而平静的统治期间，与远方港口的贸易十分繁荣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rule</td><td>/ˈruːl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>规则，规定</td><td>break the rules<br>违反规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Anyone who breaks the rules twice loses their membership.  任何人两次违反规定就会失去会员资格。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sew up</td><td>/soʊ ˈʌp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>完全控制，稳操胜券</td><td>sew up the deal<br>敲定交易</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company sewed up the contract before its rivals could even bid.  公司在对手投标前就敲定了合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>nirvana</b></td><td>/nɪrˈvɑːnə/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>涅槃；极乐境界</td><td>attain nirvana<br>达到极乐境界</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 For her, a quiet beach at sunset was pure nirvana.  对她来说，日落时安静的海滩就是极乐世界。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 empyrean</td><td>/ˌɛmpaɪˈriən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>最高天，天界；天空</td><td>the empyrean heights<br>天穹的高处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poet imagined his soul rising into the empyrean far above the clouds.  诗人想象自己的灵魂升入云端之上的最高天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fantasyland</td><td>/ˈfæntəsiˌlænd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>幻想世界，虚幻之地</td><td>live in fantasyland<br>生活在幻想中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Anyone expecting instant profits is living in fantasyland, the analyst warned.  分析师警告，指望立刻获利的人都生活在幻想中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heaven</td><td>/ˈhevən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>天堂，天国；极乐之境</td><td>go to heaven<br>升入天堂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After a week of rain, this sunshine feels like heaven to me.  下了一周雨后，这阳光对我如同天堂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 lotusland</del></td><td>/ˈloʊtəslænd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>安乐乡，极乐之地</td><td>a tropical lotusland<br>热带安乐乡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tourists treat the island as a lotusland where nobody ever has to work.  游客把这座岛当成无需劳作的安乐乡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 utopia</td><td>/juˈtoʊpiə/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>乌托邦，理想国</td><td>a socialist utopia<br>社会主义乌托邦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No political system has ever produced the utopia its founders promised.  没有政治制度实现过其创立者承诺的乌托邦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hell</td><td>/ˈhel/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>地狱；苦境，苦难</td><td>a living hell<br>人间地狱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The long march through the desert was sheer hell for the soldiers.  穿越沙漠的长途行军对士兵而言简直是地狱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inferno</td><td>/ɪnˈfɜːrnoʊ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>地狱般的地方；熊熊大火</td><td>a raging inferno<br>熊熊烈火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Firefighters could not enter the burning warehouse, now a roaring inferno.  消防员无法进入这座已成火海的仓库。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>nostalgia</b></td><td>/nəˈstældʒə/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>怀旧，怀念</td><td>a wave of nostalgia<br>一阵怀旧之情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A wave of nostalgia swept over her when she heard the old song.  听到那首老歌时，一阵怀旧之情涌上她的心头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reminiscence</td><td>/ˌreməˈnɪsəns/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>回忆，往事追想</td><td>childhood reminiscences<br>童年回忆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His reminiscences of wartime London filled three thick notebooks.  他对战时伦敦的回忆写满了三本厚笔记。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 oblivion</td><td>/əˈblɪviən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>遗忘，被忘却的状态</td><td>sink into oblivion<br>陷入被遗忘</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Once famous, the singer slipped quietly into oblivion after the war.  这位歌手曾红极一时，战后却悄然被人遗忘。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>notoriety</b></td><td>/ˌnoʊtəˈraɪəti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>坏名声</td><td>gain instant notoriety<br>立刻声名狼藉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The video gained him instant notoriety that followed him for the rest of his career.  这段视频让他立刻声名狼藉，并伴随他整个职业生涯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 infamy</td><td>/ˈɪnfəmi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恶名，臭名；恶行</td><td>a day of infamy<br>耻辱的一天</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His name lives on in infamy after the scandal.  丑闻之后他的名字以恶名流传下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 obloquy</del></td><td>/ˈɑːbləkwi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>谩骂，诽谤</td><td>suffer public obloquy<br>遭受公众谩骂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The former minister suffered public obloquy for years after the scandal broke.  丑闻爆发后，这位前部长多年遭受公众谩骂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 odium</td><td>/ˈoʊdiəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>憎恶，讨厌</td><td>public odium<br>公众的憎恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Public odium followed the executives long after the company had been dissolved.  公司解散很久之后，公众的憎恶仍伴随着这些高管。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 opprobrium</td><td>/əˈproʊbriəm/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>公开谴责，耻辱</td><td>public opprobrium<br>公众的谴责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor faced weeks of opprobrium after the decision.  市长在那个决定之后面对数周的谴责。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 anonymity</td><td>/ˌænəˈnɪmɪti/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>匿名；默默无闻</td><td>preserve one's anonymity<br>保持匿名</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The donor insisted on complete anonymity when giving the museum his collection.  捐赠者把藏品赠给博物馆时坚持完全匿名。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 oblivion</td><td>/əˈblɪviən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>遗忘，被忘却的状态</td><td>sink into oblivion<br>陷入被遗忘</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Once famous, the singer slipped quietly into oblivion after the war.  这位歌手曾红极一时，战后却悄然被人遗忘。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 obscurity</td><td>/əbˈskjʊrəti/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>晦涩难懂；默默无闻</td><td>live in obscurity<br>默默无闻地生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poet died in obscurity but is famous today.  这位诗人默默无闻地去世，如今却很有名。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>nourish</b></td><td>/ˈnɜːrɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>培养，促进</td><td>nourish young talent<br>培养年轻人才</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The foundation exists to nourish young talent in music and the visual arts.  这家基金会的存在是为了培养音乐和视觉艺术方面的年轻人才。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 advance</td><td>/ədˈvæns/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>前进；促进；预付款</td><td>advance a theory<br>提出一种理论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The army advanced slowly through the thick forest at dawn.  黎明时军队缓慢穿过密林前进。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cultivate</td><td>/ˈkʌltɪveɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>提升，加强；种植，培养</td><td>cultivate a friendship<br>培养一段友谊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She cultivated a wide network of contacts during her years abroad.  她在国外的岁月里培养了广泛的人际关系网。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 encourage</td><td>/ɪnˈkɜːrɪdʒ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>鼓励，激励；促进</td><td>encourage students to read<br>鼓励学生阅读</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Good teachers encourage students to ask difficult questions.  好老师鼓励学生提出有难度的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 forward</td><td>/ˈfɔːrwərd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>向前的；前部的</td><td>take a forward step<br>向前迈一步</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He leaned forward to hear the quiet speaker better.  他向前倾身以便听清声音轻的讲话者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 further</td><td>/ˈfɜːrðər/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>促进，推动；更远的</td><td>further his career<br>促进他的事业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She took the job abroad to further her research on glaciers.  她接受海外工作以推进她的冰川研究。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 incubate</td><td>/ˈɪŋkjubeɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>孵化；帮助，培养，促进</td><td>incubate a startup<br>孵化一家初创公司</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The university incubates dozens of small companies every single year.  这所大学每年孵化数十家小公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 promote</td><td>/prəˈmoʊt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>促进，助长；晋升</td><td>promote economic growth<br>促进经济增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The campaign aims to promote healthy eating among children.  该活动旨在促进儿童健康饮食。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 discourage</td><td>/dɪsˈkɜːrɪdʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使沮丧，使气馁；劝阻</td><td>discourage him from trying<br>劝他不要尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Early failure should not discourage a young researcher.  早期的失败不该让年轻研究者气馁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 frustrate</td><td>/ˈfrʌstreɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阻挠，使受挫；使沮丧</td><td>frustrate one's plans<br>使计划受挫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy snow frustrated our plans to drive home.  大雪打乱了我们开车回家的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hinder</td><td>/ˈhɪndər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阻碍，妨碍</td><td>hinder progress<br>妨碍进展</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Poor lighting hindered the search for survivors.  照明不足妨碍了对生还者的搜寻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inhibit</td><td>/ɪnˈhɪbɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抑制，阻碍</td><td>inhibit the growth of bacteria<br>抑制细菌生长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cold weather inhibits the growth of these tropical plants.  寒冷天气抑制了这些热带植物的生长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>novice</b></td><td>/ˈnɑːvɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>新手，初学者</td><td>a complete novice<br>彻底的新手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A complete novice, he had never held a chisel before that afternoon.  他是个彻底的新手，那天下午之前从没握过凿子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apprentice</td><td>/əˈprentəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>学徒，新手</td><td>a carpenter's apprentice<br>木匠学徒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He started as an apprentice and now owns the shop.  他从学徒做起，现在拥有这家店。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 colt</td><td>/ˈkoʊlt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>新手，生手</td><td>an untried colt<br>没经验的新手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team's colts learned fast under the veteran captain.  在老队长带领下队里的新手学得很快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fledgling</td><td>/ˈfledʒlɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>新手</td><td>a fledgling company<br>新兴的公司</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fledgling airline struggled through its very first winter.  这家新兴航空公司艰难度过了第一个冬天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 freshman</td><td>/ˈfreʃmən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>大一新生；新手</td><td>a freshman senator<br>新任参议员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a freshman he knew almost nobody on campus.  作为大一新生，他在校园里几乎谁也不认识。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 greenhorn</td><td>/ˈɡriːnhɔrn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>新手，生手</td><td>a complete greenhorn<br>十足的新手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a greenhorn he asked questions the veterans found obvious.  作为新手，他问的问题老手们觉得显而易见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 neophyte</td><td>/ˈniːəfaɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>初学者，新手</td><td>a neophyte in politics<br>政界的新手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a neophyte in politics, he underestimated how slowly legislation actually moves.  作为政界的新手，他低估了立法进程实际有多缓慢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 newcomer</td><td>/ˈnuːˌkʌmər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>新来者，新手</td><td>a newcomer to the field<br>该领域的新人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a newcomer to the company, she asked plenty of sensible questions.  作为公司新人，她提了很多合理的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 recruit</td><td>/rəˈkruːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>新成员，新兵；招募</td><td>a raw recruit<br>毫无经验的新兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every new recruit spends six weeks in basic training.  每名新兵都要接受六周基础训练。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rook</td><td>/ˈruːk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>新手，生手；骗取</td><td>a raw rook<br>十足的生手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sergeant had little patience with a rook who could not march.  中士对不会行军的新兵没什么耐心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tenderfoot</td><td>/ˈtendərfʊt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>新手，生手</td><td>a mountain tenderfoot<br>登山新手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a tenderfoot he packed twice the gear he needed.  作为新手，他带了两倍于所需的装备。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tyro</td><td>/ˈtaɪroʊ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>新手，业余爱好者</td><td>a tyro at chess<br>国际象棋新手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a tyro at chess, he lost every game that afternoon.  作为国际象棋新手，他那天下午每局都输了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 virgin</td><td>/ˈvɜːrdʒɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>未开发的，原始的</td><td>virgin forest<br>原始森林</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Loggers finally reached the last virgin forest in the valley.  伐木者终于抵达谷中最后一片原始森林。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 doyen</td><td>/ˈdɔɪən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>资深人士，元老</td><td>the doyen of critics<br>批评界的元老</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 For thirty years he was the doyen of critics at that newspaper.  三十年来他一直是那家报纸批评界的元老。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 maven</td><td>/ˈmeɪvn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>专家</td><td>a marketing maven<br>营销专家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magazine hired a marketing maven to explain why the campaign had failed.  杂志请来一位营销专家解释这次宣传为何失败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 veteran</td><td>/ˈvetərən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>老兵；有丰富经验的人</td><td>a veteran journalist<br>资深记者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A veteran journalist, she has covered four wars and six elections.  作为资深记者，她报道过四场战争和六次选举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>nullification</b></td><td>/ˌnʌlɪfiˈkeɪʃn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（尤指法律条文等正式的）废除，废弃</td><td>the nullification of the treaty<br>条约的废除</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nullification of the treaty left both governments without any legal framework.  条约的废除使两国政府失去了任何法律框架。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abolishment</td><td>/əˈbɑlɪʃmənt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>废除，取消</td><td>the abolishment of slavery<br>奴隶制的废除</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The abolishment of the old tax rules took effect last January.  旧税法的废除于去年一月生效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abrogation</td><td>/ˌæbrəˈɡeɪʃən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（法令、条约的）废止</td><td>abrogation of the treaty<br>条约的废止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics condemned the abrogation of workers' rights under the new law.  批评者谴责新法废除了工人的权利。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 annulment</td><td>/əˈnʌlmənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>宣告无效，废止</td><td>annulment of the marriage<br>婚姻的废止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She sought an annulment of the marriage after only two months.  她仅两个月后就申请宣告婚姻无效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cancellation</td><td>/ˌkænsəˈleɪʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>取消，撤销</td><td>last-minute cancellation<br>临时取消</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy snow forced the cancellation of every afternoon flight.  大雪迫使下午所有航班取消。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 invalidation</td><td>/ˌɪnvæləˈdeɪʃən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>使无效，作废</td><td>invalidation of the contract<br>合同的作废</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court ordered the invalidation of all ballots signed in pencil.  法院裁定所有用铅笔签署的选票无效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 negation</td><td>/nɪˈɡeɪʃn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>否定</td><td>the negation of freedom<br>对自由的否定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Censorship of this kind amounts to the negation of freedom of thought.  这种审查等于是对思想自由的否定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 repeal</td><td>/rɪˈpiːl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>撤销，废除（法律等）</td><td>repeal the old law<br>废除旧法律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lawmakers voted to repeal the outdated statute that had confused courts for decades.  议员们投票废除了这部困扰法院数十年的过时法规。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 rescindment</del></td><td>/rɪˈsɪndmənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>撤销，废除</td><td>rescindment of the order<br>命令的撤销</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lawyers argued for the rescindment of the disputed regulation.  律师主张撤销这项有争议的法规。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 voiding</td><td>/ˈvɔɪdɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>使失效，作废</td><td>voiding of the warranty<br>保修的失效</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Opening the case results in the voiding of your warranty.  打开机壳会导致保修失效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 enactment</td><td>/ɪˈnæktmənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>制定，颁布（法律）</td><td>the enactment of the law<br>法律的颁布</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The enactment of the new law took almost four years.  这部新法的颁布用了将近四年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 legislation</td><td>/ˌledʒɪsˈleɪʃən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>立法；法律条文</td><td>pass new legislation<br>通过新法律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Congress passed sweeping legislation to protect coastal wetlands.  国会通过了保护沿海湿地的重大立法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>numb</b></td><td>/nʌm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>麻木的，无感情的</td><td>numb with grief<br>因悲伤而麻木</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Numb with grief, she answered the reporters' questions in a flat voice.  她因悲伤而麻木，用平板的声音回答记者的提问。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 affectless</del></td><td>/ˈæfektləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无情感的，冷漠的</td><td>an affectless voice<br>毫无感情的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness described the crime in a flat, affectless tone.  证人用平淡无情感的语调描述这桩罪行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apathetic</td><td>/ˌæpəˈθetɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷漠的，无兴趣的</td><td>apathetic voters<br>冷漠的选民</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most students seemed apathetic about the election and did not vote.  大多数学生对这次选举漠不关心，也没有投票。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 catatonic</td><td>/ˌkætəˈtɑːnɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>呆滞的，木僵的</td><td>a catatonic stare<br>呆滞的目光</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After the news he sat in a catatonic silence.  听到消息后他呆滞地默坐着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deadpan</td><td>/ˈdedpæn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>不带表情的，板着脸的</td><td>a deadpan expression<br>面无表情的神情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He told the joke with a deadpan face and nobody laughed.  他面无表情地讲了那个笑话，没有人笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 emotionless</td><td>/ɪˈmoʊʃənləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无感情的，冷漠的</td><td>an emotionless voice<br>毫无感情的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He read the verdict in a flat, emotionless tone.  他用平板毫无感情的语调宣读了判决。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impassible</td><td>/ɪmˈpæsəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无痛感的，不动感情的</td><td>an impassible expression<br>毫无表情的神色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The guard stood impassible while the crowd shouted insults.  人群叫骂时，卫兵毫无表情地站着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 passionless</td><td>/ˈpæʃənləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷淡的，无激情的</td><td>a passionless performance<br>毫无激情的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critic dismissed it as a technically perfect but passionless performance.  评论家称之为技术完美却毫无激情的表演。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 phlegmatic</td><td>/fleɡˈmætɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷静的，无感情的，淡漠的</td><td>a phlegmatic temperament<br>冷静的性情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her phlegmatic response to the crisis calmed the entire staff.  她对危机的冷静反应让全体员工镇定下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stoic</td><td>/ˈstoʊɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>隐忍的，冷静的</td><td>stoic acceptance of pain<br>隐忍地承受痛苦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her stoic acceptance of pain impressed even the veteran nurses.  她隐忍地承受痛苦，连资深护士都为之动容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stolid</td><td>/ˈstɑːlɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无动于衷的，感情麻木的</td><td>a stolid unsmiling guard<br>麻木不笑的卫兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A stolid unsmiling guard stood beside the gate all afternoon.  一名麻木不笑的卫兵整个下午守在门旁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 demonstrative</td><td>/dɪˈmɑːnstrətɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>感情外露的</td><td>a demonstrative greeting<br>热情外露的问候</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her demonstrative hugs surprised colleagues used to formal handshakes.  她热情的拥抱让习惯正式握手的同事很惊讶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 emotional</td><td>/ɪˈmoʊʃənəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>感情丰富的；动情的</td><td>an emotional farewell speech<br>动情的告别演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He became emotional when describing his father's last letter.  描述父亲最后一封信时他动了感情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fervent</td><td>/ˈfɜːrvənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>充满感情的，热情洋溢的</td><td>a fervent believer<br>热切的信徒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is a fervent believer in free public libraries for everyone.  她热切地相信应该为所有人提供免费的公共图书馆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fervid</td><td>/ˈfɜːrvɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>酷热的；热情的</td><td>a fervid supporter<br>热情的支持者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His fervid speech moved even the quietest people in the room.  他热情洋溢的演讲连房间里最安静的人都被感动了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impassioned</td><td>/ɪmˈpæʃənd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>慷慨激昂的，充满激情的</td><td>an impassioned speech<br>慷慨激昂的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave an impassioned defense of public libraries.  她慷慨激昂地为公共图书馆辩护。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 passionate</td><td>/ˈpæʃənət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>热情的，充满激情的</td><td>a passionate defense of art<br>对艺术的热情辩护</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is passionate about protecting the city's old neighborhoods.  她对保护城市的老街区充满热情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vehement</td><td>/ˈviːəmənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（情感）强烈的，热情的</td><td>vehement opposition<br>强烈的反对</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She made a vehement protest against the new tax.  她对新税提出了强烈的抗议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 5

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>oaf</b></td><td>/oʊf/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>愚蠢的人</td><td>a clumsy oaf<br>笨手笨脚的蠢人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Some clumsy oaf knocked over the display and walked away without apologizing.  某个笨手笨脚的蠢人撞倒了展台，一句道歉也没有就走了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 airhead</td><td>/ˈerˌhed/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>笨蛋，没脑子的人</td><td>act like an airhead<br>表现得像个笨蛋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not treat her like an airhead; she reads constantly.  别把她当笨蛋，她一直在读书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dolt</td><td>/doʊlt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>笨蛋</td><td>call him a dolt<br>骂他是笨蛋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not call him a dolt simply because he asked an obvious question.  别只因为他问了个显而易见的问题就骂他笨蛋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dope</td><td>/ˈdoʊp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>蠢人，笨蛋；毒品</td><td>a total dope<br>十足的蠢货</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a dope would leave his passport in a taxi.  只有蠢人才会把护照忘在出租车里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dullard</td><td>/ˈdʌlərd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>笨蛋</td><td>a hopeless dullard<br>无可救药的笨蛋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher wrongly treated the quiet boy as a hopeless dullard.  老师错把这个安静的男孩当成无可救药的笨蛋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 dumbhead</del></td><td>/ˈdʌmhɛd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>傻瓜，笨蛋</td><td>a hopeless dumbhead<br>无可救药的笨蛋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Stop acting like a dumbhead and read the instructions carefully.  别像个笨蛋，仔细读一下说明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fool</td><td>/ˈfuːl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>傻瓜，蠢人；愚弄</td><td>make a fool of oneself<br>让自己出丑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt like a fool after believing the obvious lie.  信了那个明显的谎言后，他觉得自己像个傻瓜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 idiot</td><td>/ˈɪdiət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>白痴，蠢人</td><td>act like an idiot<br>表现得像蠢人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only an idiot would drive that fast in fog.  只有蠢人才会在雾中开那么快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imbecile</td><td>/ˈɪmbəsəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>蠢人，白痴；愚蠢的</td><td>an utter imbecile<br>十足的白痴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Any imbecile could see that the old bridge was unsafe.  任何蠢人都能看出这座旧桥不安全。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 moron</td><td>/ˈmɔːˌrɑːn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>蠢人，笨蛋</td><td>some moron on the road<br>路上某个蠢货</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Some moron parked across two spaces again.  又有某个蠢货横占了两个车位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 simpleton</td><td>/ˈsɪmpltən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>（缺乏常识的）笨蛋</td><td>treat him as a simpleton<br>把他当傻子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They treated the new clerk as a simpleton until he corrected their figures.  他们把新来的职员当傻子，直到他改正了他们的数字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 brain</td><td>/ˈbreɪn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>聪明人，智囊；大脑</td><td>the brains of the operation<br>行动的智囊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was the brains behind the company's most profitable product.  她是公司最赚钱产品背后的智囊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intellectual</td><td>/ˌɪntəˈlektʃuəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>知识分子；智力的</td><td>a public intellectual<br>公共知识分子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She became a leading intellectual in the reform movement.  她成为改革运动中的主要知识分子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sage</td><td>/seɪdʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>智者</td><td>an ancient sage<br>古代的智者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Villagers walked for days to ask the old sage for advice.  村民们走了好几天去向那位老智者求教。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wit</td><td>/wɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>机智，智慧</td><td>a flash of wit<br>一闪的机智</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her quick wit saved the interview after the microphone failed.  麦克风出故障后，是她的机敏救了那场访谈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>observant</b></td><td>/əbˈzɜːrvənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>警觉的；严格遵守规章的</td><td>an observant guard<br>警觉的守卫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An observant guard noticed the broken lock before the thieves could return.  一名警觉的守卫在窃贼返回前注意到了被弄坏的锁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 alert</td><td>/əˈlɜːrt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>警觉的，机敏的；警报</td><td>stay alert<br>保持警觉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Night guards must stay alert even during the quietest hours.  夜间守卫即便在最安静的时段也必须保持警觉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 attentive</td><td>/əˈtentɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>留心的，专注的；周到的</td><td>an attentive audience<br>专注的听众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nurse stayed attentive to every change in his breathing.  护士留心他呼吸的每一点变化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 awake</td><td>/əˈweɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>醒着的；唤醒</td><td>lie awake at night<br>夜里醒着躺着</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The noise from the street kept me awake until dawn.  街上的噪音让我一直醒到黎明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cautious</td><td>/ˈkɔːʃəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>谨慎的，小心的</td><td>a cautious approach<br>谨慎的做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investors remained cautious until the election results were announced.  在选举结果公布前投资者一直很谨慎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sharp</td><td>/ˈʃɑːrp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>尖刻的；锋利的；敏锐的</td><td>a sharp tongue<br>说话尖刻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critic has a sharp tongue but an unusually fair mind.  这位评论家言辞尖刻，心思却格外公正。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vigilant</td><td>/ˈvɪdʒɪlənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>警醒的，警惕的</td><td>remain vigilant<br>保持警惕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Guards must remain vigilant throughout the long and quiet night shift.  守卫在漫长安静的夜班中必须始终保持警惕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 watchful</td><td>/ˈwɑːtʃfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>警惕的，注意的</td><td>a watchful eye<br>警惕的目光</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children played under their grandmother's watchful eye.  孩子们在祖母警惕的注视下玩耍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 law-abiding</td><td>/ˈlɔ əˌbaɪdɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>守法的</td><td>law-abiding citizens<br>守法公民</td></tr>
