<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 echo</td><td>/ˈekoʊ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>附和，重复；回声</td><td>echo his opinion<br>附和他的意见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Younger delegates simply echoed the governor's opinion.  年轻代表只是附和州长的意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 reecho</del></td><td>/riˈɛkoʊ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词、及物动词</td><td>反复回响，再起回声</td><td>reecho off the cliffs<br>在崖壁间回响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The gunshot reechoed among the narrow canyon walls.  枪声在狭窄的峡谷壁间回响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 resonate</td><td>/ˈrezəneɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>引起共鸣，产生反响</td><td>resonate with readers<br>在读者中引起共鸣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her speech resonated with everyone who had lost a job.  她的演讲在每个失业过的人心中引起共鸣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 resound</td><td>/rɪˈzaʊnd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>回响；被赞颂</td><td>resound with praise<br>充满赞颂之声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hall resounded with applause for the young violinist.  大厅回响着对这位年轻小提琴手的掌声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>rhetorical</b></td><td>/rɪˈtɔːrɪkl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>言辞华丽（却往往无内涵）的</td><td>empty rhetorical flourishes<br>空洞的华丽辞藻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His speech was full of rhetorical flourishes but offered no concrete plan.  他的演讲满是华丽辞藻，却没有提出任何具体计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bombastic</td><td>/bɑːmˈbæstɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>夸大的，浮夸的</td><td>bombastic rhetoric<br>浮夸的辞令</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His bombastic speech impressed nobody in the audience.  他浮夸的演讲没有打动任何听众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flatulent</td><td>/ˈflætʃələnt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>浮夸的，空洞的</td><td>flatulent rhetoric<br>浮夸的辞藻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His flatulent speech impressed nobody in the audience.  他浮夸的演讲没能打动任何听众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 florid</td><td>/ˈflɔːrɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>辞藻华丽的，花哨的</td><td>florid prose<br>辞藻华丽的散文</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critic mocked the florid prose of the young novelist.  评论家嘲讽这位年轻小说家辞藻华丽的文风。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fustian</td><td>/ˈfʌstʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>浮夸的言辞；夸张的</td><td>empty fustian<br>空洞的浮夸言辞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His speech was pure fustian, loud but entirely without substance.  他的演讲纯属浮夸，声大而毫无实质。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gaseous</td><td>/ˈɡæsiəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>空洞浮夸的；气态的</td><td>gaseous rhetoric<br>空洞浮夸的辞令</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reporters dismissed the minister's gaseous promises about future reform.  记者不理会部长关于改革的空洞承诺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gassy</td><td>/ˈɡæsi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>空话连篇的，夸夸其谈的</td><td>a gassy speaker<br>夸夸其谈的演讲者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The gassy editorial took two pages to say nothing.  这篇空话连篇的社论用两页什么也没说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grandiloquent</td><td>/ɡrænˈdɪləkwənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>夸张的，说大话的</td><td>grandiloquent promises<br>夸张的许诺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Voters grew tired of his grandiloquent promises about reform.  选民厌倦了他关于改革的夸张许诺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 oratorical</td><td>/ˌɔːrəˈtɔːrəkəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>演说的，讲演术的</td><td>oratorical skill<br>演讲技巧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lincoln's oratorical power still impresses readers of his speeches.  林肯的演说力量至今仍打动读者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 orotund</del></td><td>/ˈɔrəˌtʌnd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（声音）洪亮的；夸张浮华的</td><td>an orotund delivery<br>浑厚夸张的演讲腔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The senator's orotund phrases impressed nobody in the small room.  参议员浮夸洪亮的措辞在这小房间里没打动任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 purple</td><td>/ˈpɜːrpəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（文风）华丽夸张的；紫的</td><td>purple prose<br>辞藻华丽的文章</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Editors cut the purple passages from his travel essays.  编辑删掉了他游记中辞藻过度华丽的段落。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 windy</td><td>/ˈwɪndi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冗长的</td><td>a windy speech<br>冗长空洞的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His windy speech said very little in more than an hour.  他冗长的演讲讲了一个多小时却几乎没什么内容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prosaic</td><td>/prəˈzeɪɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调的，常见的</td><td>prosaic details<br>平淡的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Behind the legend lies a prosaic story about a lost receipt.  传说背后是一个关于遗失收据的平淡故事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>rigmarole</b></td><td>/ˈrɪɡməroʊl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>混乱而无意义的话</td><td>the usual rigmarole<br>一贯的繁琐废话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His explanation was a long rigmarole that nobody could follow.  他的解释是一大段混乱无意义的话，没人听得懂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abracadabra</td><td>/ˌæbrəkəˈdæbrə/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>咒语；胡言乱语</td><td>mutter abracadabra<br>念念咒语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magician waved his wand and shouted abracadabra.  魔术师挥动魔杖，喊出咒语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 babble</td><td>/ˈbæbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>发出含糊无意义的嘟囔声，胡乱说</td><td>babble on about nothing<br>没完没了地瞎扯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The toddler would babble happily for hours in his crib.  这个学步的孩子会在婴儿床里开心地咿呀乱说几个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 drivel</td><td>/ˈdrɪvl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>胡言乱语</td><td>talk complete drivel<br>满口胡言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pamphlet talked complete drivel about curing serious illness with colored light.  那本小册子满口胡言，说用彩色光能治重病。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gabble</td><td>/ˈɡæbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>急促而含糊地说</td><td>gabble away nervously<br>紧张地急促乱说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The child gabbled excitedly about everything he had seen that day.  孩子兴奋地急促讲述那天见到的一切。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gibber</td><td>/ˈdʒɪbər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>语无伦次地快说</td><td>gibber with fright<br>吓得语无伦次</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was gibbering with terror and no one could understand him.  他吓得语无伦次，没人能听懂他说什么。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jabber</td><td>/ˈdʒæbər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>快而不清楚地说</td><td>jabber away in Italian<br>用意大利语急促地说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tourists jabbered away in Italian throughout the entire bus ride.  游客们在整段车程中都用意大利语喋喋不休。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nonsense</td><td>/ˈnɑːnsens/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>胡说，废话</td><td>talk nonsense<br>说废话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most of what he wrote in that letter was pure nonsense.  他在那封信里写的大多是纯粹的胡话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prattle</td><td>/ˈprætəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>闲扯，唠叨</td><td>prattle on for hours<br>唠叨好几个小时</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She prattled happily about her garden while we drank our tea.  我们喝茶时她开心地闲扯她的花园。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ruthless</b></td><td>/ˈruːθləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>没有怜悯心的，残忍的</td><td>a ruthless dictator<br>残忍无情的独裁者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The general was ruthless with anyone who questioned his orders.  将军对任何质疑其命令的人都残忍无情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cruel</td><td>/ˈkruːəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>残酷的，无情的</td><td>a cruel joke<br>残忍的玩笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was cruel to leave the dog out in the storm.  把狗留在暴风雨里太残忍了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heartless</td><td>/ˈhɑːrtləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无情的，狠心的</td><td>a heartless decision<br>无情的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was heartless to fire her the week before Christmas.  在圣诞节前一周解雇她真是无情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inhumane</td><td>/ˌɪnhjuˈmeɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不人道的，残忍的</td><td>inhumane treatment of prisoners<br>对囚犯的残忍对待</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics condemned the inhumane conditions inside the overcrowded shelter.  批评者谴责了过度拥挤的收容所内不人道的条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 ironfisted</del></td><td>/ˈaɪərnˌfɪstɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>铁腕的，严厉无情的</td><td>ironfisted rule<br>铁腕统治</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The colony was run by an ironfisted governor for thirty years.  这块殖民地被一位铁腕总督统治了三十年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 iron-hearted</td><td>/ˈaɪərn ˈhɑrtɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>铁石心肠的，冷酷的</td><td>an iron-hearted judge<br>铁石心肠的法官</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The iron-hearted landlord evicted the family in midwinter.  铁石心肠的房东在隆冬赶走了这家人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pitiless</td><td>/ˈpɪtiləs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>没有同情心的</td><td>a pitiless judge<br>冷酷无情的法官</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pitiless judge showed no mercy to the young first offender.  那位冷酷无情的法官对年轻的初犯毫不留情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 merciless</td><td>/ˈmɜːrsələs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>毫无仁慈的，无情的</td><td>a merciless critic<br>毫不留情的批评者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The merciless summer sun beat down on the empty streets.  无情的夏日阳光炙烤着空荡的街道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mortal</td><td>/ˈmɔːrtəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>终将死亡的，凡人的；致命的</td><td>mortal beings<br>凡人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The play reminds us that even great kings are mortal men.  这部剧提醒我们连伟大的国王也是凡人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 relentless</td><td>/rɪˈlentləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>残酷的，无情的；固执的，不肯妥协的</td><td>relentless pressure<br>无休止的压力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Relentless pressure from creditors finally forced the small firm to close.  债权人无情的压力最终迫使这家小公司关门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 remorseless</td><td>/rɪˈmɔːrsləs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>毫无悔意的，冷酷无情的</td><td>remorseless cruelty<br>冷酷无情的残忍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The killer remained remorseless throughout the long public trial.  凶手在漫长的公开审判中始终毫无悔意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 charitable</td><td>/ˈtʃærətəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>宽容的，仁慈的</td><td>a charitable view<br>宽容的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He took a charitable view of his student's careless mistake.  他对学生的粗心错误持宽容态度。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 compassionate</td><td>/kəmˈpæʃənət/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有同情心的，慈悲的</td><td>compassionate care<br>充满关怀的照护</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A compassionate nurse sat with the frightened child all night.  一位有同情心的护士整夜陪着那个受惊的孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humane</td><td>/hjuˈmeɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>人道的，仁慈的</td><td>humane treatment of prisoners<br>人道地对待囚犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Activists demanded more humane conditions inside the detention center.  活动人士要求改善拘留中心内的人道条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 kindhearted</td><td>/ˌkaɪndˈhɑrtɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好心的，仁慈的</td><td>a kindhearted neighbor<br>好心的邻居</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A kindhearted stranger paid for the child's bus ticket.  一位好心的陌生人替那孩子付了车票。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 merciful</td><td>/ˈmɜːrsɪfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>仁慈的，宽厚的</td><td>a merciful judge<br>宽仁的法官</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court was merciful and gave him only a small fine.  法庭很宽仁，只罚了他少许款项。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sympathetic</td><td>/ˌsɪmpəˈθetɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>同情的，有同情心的</td><td>a sympathetic listener<br>富有同情心的倾听者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nurse gave her a sympathetic look and held her hand.  护士同情地看着她，握住了她的手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tender</td><td>/ˈtendər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>温柔的，心软同情的；正式提出</td><td>a tender heart<br>一颗温柔同情的心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has a tender heart and cannot bear to see animals suffer.  她心地温软，见不得动物受苦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>screen</b></td><td>/skriːn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>掩护物，屏障；掩护，遮蔽</td><td>a screen of trees<br>一道树木屏障</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A thick screen of trees hid the old house from the road.  一道浓密的树木屏障把老屋从路上遮住了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aegis</td><td>/ˈiːdʒəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>保护，庇护</td><td>under the aegis of<br>在……的庇护下</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The talks continued under the aegis of the United Nations.  会谈在联合国的主持庇护下继续。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ammunition</td><td>/ˌæmjəˈnɪʃən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>弹药；论战的材料</td><td>run out of ammunition<br>弹药用尽</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The soldiers carried enough ammunition for a three-day defense.  士兵携带了足够守三天的弹药。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 armor</td><td>/ˈɑːrmər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>盔甲，防护装备</td><td>a suit of armor<br>一套盔甲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The knight's armor weighed nearly as much as he did.  骑士的盔甲几乎和他本人一样重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cover</td><td>/ˈkʌvər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>掩盖，遮盖；覆盖物</td><td>cover up the truth<br>掩盖真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They tried to cover up the truth with a vague statement.  他们试图用含糊的声明掩盖真相。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 guard</td><td>/ˈɡɑːrd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>保护；警卫</td><td>guard against loss<br>防范损失</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two dogs guard the warehouse every night.  两条狗每晚守卫这座仓库。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 protection</td><td>/prəˈtekʃən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>保护，防护</td><td>protection from the wind<br>挡风的防护</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thin tent offered little protection against the freezing wind.  薄帐篷几乎挡不住刺骨的寒风。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 safeguard</td><td>/ˈseɪfɡɑːrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>保护措施</td><td>a legal safeguard<br>法律保障</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new rules act as a safeguard against careless lending.  新规定是对草率放贷的一道保障。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 security</td><td>/sɪˈkjʊrəti/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>安全，保障</td><td>a sense of security<br>安全感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new fence gave the family a real sense of security.  新围栏给这家人带来真正的安全感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shield</td><td>/ˈʃiːld/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>保护，遮挡；盾</td><td>shield one's eyes<br>遮住眼睛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She raised a hand to shield her eyes from the glare.  她举手遮挡刺眼的强光。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wall</td><td>/ˈwɔːl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>墙，壁；用墙围住</td><td>a brick wall<br>砖墙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Photographs of his children covered the wall behind the desk.  书桌后的墙上贴满了他孩子的照片。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ward</td><td>/ˈwɔːrd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>守护，挡开；病房</td><td>ward off danger<br>挡开危险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Garlic was once used to ward off illness.  大蒜曾被用来抵挡疾病。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 belie</td><td>/bɪˈlaɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>与…相对立；与…相矛盾</td><td>belie his claims<br>与他的说法矛盾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company's own records belie his claims about the accident.  公司自己的记录与他关于事故的说法相矛盾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cloak</td><td>/kloʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>遮掩，隐藏</td><td>cloak the truth<br>掩盖真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Officials cloaked the truth behind pages of confusing statistics.  官员用一堆令人困惑的统计数字掩盖真相。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conceal</td><td>/kənˈsiːl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>隐藏，隐瞒</td><td>conceal the truth<br>隐瞒真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness tried to conceal the truth from the police.  证人试图向警方隐瞒真相。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 curtain</td><td>/ˈkɜːrtən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>遮蔽，掩盖；窗帘</td><td>curtain off the room<br>用帘子隔开房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy drapes curtained the windows and hid the room from view.  厚重的帷幔遮住窗户，把房间藏了起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disguise</td><td>/dɪsˈɡaɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>伪装，掩饰；伪装</td><td>disguise his intentions<br>掩饰他的意图</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He disguised his disappointment behind a polite and steady smile.  他用礼貌平静的微笑掩饰了失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 enshroud</del></td><td>/ɪnˈʃraʊd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>遮蔽，笼罩</td><td>enshroud in mist<br>笼罩在雾中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thick fog enshrouded the harbor by early evening.  傍晚时分浓雾笼罩了港口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mask</td><td>/ˈmæsk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>掩盖，掩饰；面具</td><td>mask his anger<br>掩饰他的怒气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Loud music masked the sound of the approaching helicopter.  响亮的音乐掩盖了直升机靠近的声音。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obscure</td><td>/əbˈskjʊr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>含义模糊的；平凡的，不知名的</td><td>an obscure poet<br>不知名的诗人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained an obscure poet until a critic rediscovered his work decades later.  他一直是个不知名的诗人，直到几十年后一位批评家重新发现他的作品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 occult</td><td>/əˈkʌlt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以理解的</td><td>occult knowledge<br>玄奥的知识</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manuscript is filled with occult knowledge that scholars still cannot decipher.  这份手稿满是学者至今无法解读的玄奥知识。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shroud</td><td>/ʃraʊd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>将…从视线中隔离，遮蔽</td><td>shrouded in mist<br>笼罩在雾中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thick mist shrouded the harbor and hid the ships from view.  浓雾笼罩港口，把船只从视线中遮住。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 veil</td><td>/ˈveɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>遮掩，掩饰；面纱</td><td>veil his true motives<br>掩饰真实动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Polite words could not veil his true motives for long.  客气话没能长久掩饰他的真实动机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disclose</td><td>/dɪsˈkloʊz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭露，公开，透露</td><td>disclose the details<br>公开细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company refused to disclose the details of the settlement.  公司拒绝公开和解协议的细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 expose</td><td>/ɪkˈspoʊz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭露；使暴露</td><td>expose the fraud<br>揭露欺诈行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two journalists exposed the fraud after months of quiet investigation.  两名记者经过数月暗中调查揭露了这起欺诈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reveal</td><td>/rɪˈviːl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭示，透露</td><td>reveal the details<br>透露细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company refused to reveal the details of the settlement.  公司拒绝透露和解的细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 uncloak</del></td><td>/ʌnˈkloʊk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭露，揭开真相</td><td>uncloak a conspiracy<br>揭露阴谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The journalist worked for months to uncloak the corruption scheme.  这位记者花了数月揭露这个腐败阴谋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 uncover</td><td>/ənˈkʌvər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭开，发现，揭露</td><td>uncover the evidence<br>发现证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Inspectors uncovered evidence of fraud in the accounting records.  检查人员在账目中发现了欺诈的证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unmask</td><td>/ʌnˈmæsk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭露，揭穿真面目</td><td>unmask the impostor<br>揭穿那个冒充者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The journalist unmasked the company's secret polluting practices.  记者揭露了这家公司秘密排污的做法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unveil</td><td>/ənˈveɪl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭幕，公布，揭示</td><td>unveil a new model<br>发布新款</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company will unveil a new model at the spring show.  公司将在春季展会上发布新款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>scrimp</b></td><td>/skrɪmp/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>节省；吝啬；紧缩开支</td><td>scrimp and save<br>省吃俭用</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They had to scrimp and save for years to buy the shop.  他们省吃俭用多年才买下这家店铺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conserve</td><td>/kənˈsɜːrv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>节约，保存，保护</td><td>conserve energy<br>节约能源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We must conserve water during the long summer drought.  漫长的夏季干旱期间我们必须节约用水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 economize</td><td>/ɪˈkɑːnəˌmaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>节约，节省开支</td><td>economize on fuel<br>节省燃料</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The family had to economize sharply after he lost work.  他失业后一家人不得不大幅节省。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 husband</td><td>/ˈhʌzbənd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>节俭，勤俭持家</td><td>husband his resources<br>节省他的资源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The village learned to husband its water carefully through the dry months.  村民学会了在旱季小心节省用水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pinch</td><td>/ˈpɪntʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>短缺，窘迫；捏</td><td>feel the pinch<br>感到经济窘迫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Small shops began to feel the pinch as rents rose sharply.  租金猛涨后小店铺开始感到经济窘迫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 save</td><td>/ˈseɪv/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>拯救，挽救；节省</td><td>save him from danger<br>把他从危险中救出</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lifeguard saved two swimmers from the strong current.  救生员从急流中救出两名游泳者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 skimp</td><td>/skɪmp/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>节省花费，吝惜；克扣</td><td>skimp on materials<br>在材料上省钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Builders who skimp on materials always create problems for later owners.  在材料上省钱的建筑商总会给后来的业主留下麻烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spare</td><td>/ˈsper/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>宽恕，免除；备用的</td><td>spare his life<br>饶他一命</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The victors spared everyone who surrendered before nightfall.  胜利者宽恕了所有在天黑前投降的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dissipate</td><td>/ˈdɪsɪpeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>驱散，消散；浪费</td><td>dissipate the fog<br>驱散雾气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The morning mist dissipated as soon as the sun rose.  太阳一升起，晨雾就消散了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lavish</td><td>/ˈlævɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>奢侈的，大量的，无节制的；挥霍，浪费</td><td>a lavish banquet<br>奢华的宴会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They hosted a lavish banquet for three hundred guests in the garden.  他们在花园里为三百位客人举办了一场奢华的宴会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 squander</td><td>/ˈskwɑːndər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>浪费</td><td>squander an opportunity<br>浪费机会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not squander this opportunity to study abroad for a year.  不要浪费这次出国留学一年的机会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 waste</td><td>/ˈweɪst/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词、动词</td><td>荒芜的；浪费；废物</td><td>waste land<br>荒地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing grows on this waste ground beside the old factory.  旧工厂旁的这片荒地上什么也长不出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>sectarianism</b></td><td>/sekˈteriənɪzəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>宗派主义，门户之见</td><td>religious sectarianism<br>宗教派别之争</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sectarianism divided the small town for three generations.  宗派主义把这个小镇分裂了三代人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dogmatism</td><td>/ˈdɑːɡməˌtɪzəm/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>教条主义，武断</td><td>rigid dogmatism<br>僵化的教条主义</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Scientific progress suffers when dogmatism replaces careful argument.  当教条主义取代严谨论证，科学进步就受损。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 illiberalism</del></td><td>/ɪˈlɪbərəlɪzəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>狭隘，不宽容</td><td>growing illiberalism<br>日益加剧的不宽容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new censorship laws revealed the government's deepening illiberalism.  新审查法暴露了政府日益加深的不宽容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intolerance</td><td>/ɪnˈtɑːlərəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>不宽容，偏狭</td><td>religious intolerance<br>宗教上的不宽容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Intolerance of dissent slowly destroyed the club's friendly atmosphere.  对异议的不宽容慢慢毁掉了俱乐部的友好气氛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 narrow-mindedness</td><td>/ˈnæroʊ ˈmaɪndɪdnəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>心胸狭窄，见识浅薄</td><td>sheer narrow-mindedness<br>纯粹的心胸狭窄</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their narrow-mindedness kept the village suspicious of every visitor.  他们的心胸狭窄使村子对每个来客都怀疑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 partisanship</td><td>/ˈpɑːrtəzənˌʃɪp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>党派偏见，偏袒</td><td>bitter partisanship<br>激烈的党派之争</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Partisanship blocked every attempt to pass the compromise budget bill.  党派偏见阻碍了通过折中预算案的一切努力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 small-mindedness</td><td>/ˈsmɔl ˈmaɪndɪdnəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>气量狭小，小气</td><td>petty small-mindedness<br>琐碎的小气量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Office politics revealed a surprising amount of small-mindedness.  办公室政治暴露出惊人的气量狭小。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 broad-mindedness</td><td>/ˈbrɔd ˈmaɪndɪdnəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>心胸宽广，宽容大度</td><td>genuine broad-mindedness<br>真正的宽容大度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her broad-mindedness made her the natural mediator in disputes.  她的宽容大度使她自然成为纠纷的调解人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 liberalism</td><td>/ˈlɪbərəlɪzəm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>宽容开明；自由主义</td><td>political liberalism<br>政治自由主义</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nineteenth-century liberalism championed free trade and individual rights.  十九世纪自由主义倡导自由贸易和个人权利。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 open-mindedness</td><td>/ˌoʊpən ˈmaɪndɪdnəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>开明，思想开放</td><td>praised for his open-mindedness<br>因开明而受赞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her open-mindedness made her an unusually good listener.  她的开明使她成为格外好的倾听者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tolerance</td><td>/ˈtɑːlərəns/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>容忍，宽容</td><td>tolerance for dissent<br>对异见的容忍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The school shows little tolerance for cheating of any kind.  学校对任何作弊都毫不容忍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
